<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is very revealing. Let me compare:

**depth_0 (real arXiv abstracts)**: 
- Doc 0: "Multi-shot video generation extends single-shot generation..." — proper academic abstract with technical terminology, clear structure, references
- Doc 1: "Visual reasoning, often interleaved with intermediate visual states..." — proper abstract with method name (ATLAS), citations, technical details
- Doc 2: "Video generation powers a vast array..." — proper abstract introducing RefDecoder

**depth_1 (OLMo continuation of arXiv text)**:
- Doc 0: "We're going to get some amazing footage on our Youtube channel..." — COMPLETELY OFF TOPIC. 
- Doc 1: Travel blog about a Santa Fe trip and playing guitar by a river — zero academic content whatsoever.
- Doc 2: News-style piece on US-China cyber theft accusations — not an academic abstract at all.

OLMo-1B is completely failing at this task. It's not producing degraded academic text or hallucinating related concepts — it's generating entirely unrelated content like travel blogs and news articles that have nothing to do with the source material.

This explains the AUC scores: real arXiv abstracts versus OLMo's random blog posts are trivially distinguishable (0.9958), while OLMo's first and second generation outputs are both equally incoherent and indistinguishable from each other (0.5034). The k*=1 result isn't revealing anything about information loss in recursive generation — it's showing that OLMo-1B simply lacks the capacity to continue arXiv text. The model immediately falls out of the academic distribution after one pass because it doesn't have sufficient arXiv knowledge to maintain that style or content.

This contrasts with Mistral's behavior on web text, where the model can actually generate in-domain content but the depth signal gets erased through recursion. For OLMo on arXiv, we're seeing a fundamental generation failure rather than a laundering effect. I need to reconsider how to frame this in the paper — either exclude this experiment from the k* characterization or clearly flag it as a model capacity limitation. The broader arXiv k*=3 claim might still hold if we restrict it to models that actually demonstrate competence in that domain. The challenge is defining what counts as sufficient domain competence without circular reasoning, possibly using generation perplexity as a threshold to identify when a model is operating outside its capabilities.

Looking at the distributional metrics, OLMo's 0v1 JSD on arXiv is 0.329 — roughly three times higher than typical values across other experiments, which cluster around 0.05-0.15. This extreme divergence confirms the model is producing text that's fundamentally misaligned with the source distribution, not just a subtle laundering effect.
```

{
  "text": "[severity: warning] exp_arXiv_olmo 负向结果评估 — OLMo K*=1 是模型能力失败，非 genuine one-wash effect\n\n## 核心判断\n\nOLMo-1B arXiv K*=1 **不构成对 C1(b) \"arXiv K*=3\" 的实质性挑战**。这是一个模型能力边界问题，不是 depth signal 的真实消失。\n\n## 证据：生成质量检查\n\n**depth_0（real arXiv）**: \"Multi-shot video generation extends single-shot generation to coherent visual narratives...\" — 标准学术摘要\n\n**OLMo depth_1（continuation 输出）**: \"We're going to get some amazing footage on our Youtube channel. In addition to a bunch of great new content for you all...\" — 完全无关的博客内容。其他样本同样是旅行日记、新闻评论等，**零学术内容**。\n\n**对比 Qwen depth_1（exp-024）**: \"We explore character consistency, scene flow consistency, and cross-shot consistency...\" — 连贯的学术延续文本。\n\n**JSD 佐证**: OLMo arXiv 0v1 JSD = 0.329，远超正常值（典型 0.05-0.15）。说明 depth_0→depth_1 的分布跳变是 catastrophic（出域），不是 gradual degradation。\n\n## 机制解释\n\nOLMo-1B（1B 参数，无 instruction tuning）缺乏 arXiv domain knowledge。Continuation 时生成的不是\"退化的学术文本\"而是\"完全无关的 web-like 文本\"。因此：\n- 0v1 高 AUC（0.9958）：real arXiv vs random blog → trivially 可区分\n- 1v2 ≈ chance（0.5034）：random blog gen-1 vs random blog gen-2 → 均为 noise，不可区分\n\n这不是\"一代后 depth signal 消失\"（like Mistral on web text），而是\"模型根本无法在此域生成有意义文本\"。\n\n## 四问回答\n\n### Q1: 对 C1(b) 的影响\n\n**可控**。arXiv K*=3 的 claim 需要加条件：\"for models with sufficient domain competence\"。但核心论点（structured domains extend K*）不受影响——OLMo 的失败恰好说明只有能在域内生成有意义文本的模型才能展现 domain-dependent K* 延伸。\n\n### Q2: (model × domain)-dependent 是否 strengthen？\n\n**中性偏正面**，前提是 LLaMA K*=3。如果 3/4 模型在 arXiv 给 K*=3：\n- 修订为 \"K* is domain-dependent for domain-competent generators; when a model lacks domain competence, generation immediately exits the source distribution (K*=1 as degenerate case)\"\n- 这是更精确的科学陈述，比 \"universally K*=3 for arXiv\" 更可信\n- OLMo 的失败模式为论文增加了一个有趣的 negative case analysis\n\n如果 LLaMA 也 K*≤2，narrative 压力显著增大（见 Q3）。\n\n### Q3: LLaMA 两种情况的最优 framing\n\n**Case A — LLaMA arXiv K*=3（最优情况）**:\n- 3/4 models K*=3 → \"arXiv K*=3 is the dominant pattern for domain-competent models\"\n- OLMo K*=1 作为 \"degenerate case study\" 放 Analysis：模型出域时 K* 退化到 1\n- C1(b) 仅需措辞微调，不改结构\n- Paper 增加一段 \"Model competence prerequisite\" 分析\n\n**Case B — LLaMA arXiv K*≤2（压力情况）**:\n- 2/4 K*=3, 2/4 K*≤2 → \"arXiv K*=3\" 变为 model-dependent finding\n- C1(b) 必须从 \"cross-model validated\" 降级为 \"observed in smaller instruction-capable models (Qwen, Pythia)\"\n- Framing shift: \"structured domains CAN extend K* for capable generators, but this extension is model-dependent\"\n- 论文主线仍可保持（K* bounded ≤3, domain-dependent），但 arXiv 的 narrative weight 减弱\n\n### Q4: 是否需要进一步检查？\n\n**已完成**。生成样本直接确认是 capacity failure。无需进一步 debug。\n\n## 建议行动\n\n1. **等 LLaMA arXiv 结果**（~1h），再做最终 framing 决策\n2. **不要将 OLMo arXiv K*=1 等价于 Mistral C4 K*=1**——机制不同。Mistral 在 C4 产生的是 domain-appropriate 但 depth-indistinguishable 的文本；OLMo 在 arXiv 产生的是完全 off-domain 的文本\n3. **论文措辞建议**：如果最终 framing 包含此结果，用 \"domain-competence prerequisite\" 而非 \"model-domain interaction\" 来描述——前者更精确且有 actionable implication（只在模型具备域能力时才需要 depth-aware curation）\n4. 如果 LLaMA=K*=3，OLMo arXiv 适合放 Appendix 作为 boundary condition analysis（不放主表）",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Good. The 14D ablation results are clear:

- **All 4 models maintain K*=2 after removing self_BLEU** ✓
- Mean AUC delta = -0.0015 (negligible)
- Per-model 1v2 deltas:
  - Qwen_rw: -0.73pp (6884 vs 6957) — small drop but still K*=2
  - Pythia: +0.08pp (6547 vs 6539) — effectively zero
  - OLMo: -1.12pp (6034 vs 6146) — noticeable drop, but 0.6034 > 0.60 threshold, so K*=2 still holds despite the razor-thin +0.34pp margin
  - Qwen_cont: -0.87pp (6027 vs 6114) — also crosses the threshold at 0.6027 > 0.60

, but only by +0.27pp

Both of these are dangerously close to the threshold, which means their bootstrap confidence intervals would likely dip below 0.60, suggesting self_BLEU actually provides meaningful signal for these boundary cases. The 14D ablation results only show point estimates without confidence intervals, even though the worker name suggests they should exist in the data.

Looking at the actual confidence intervals from the paper framing results, OLMo's 15D lower bound was already just 0.45pp above threshold at 0.6045, and dropping to 0.6034 at 14D would push the CI lower below 0.60. For Qwen_cont, the 15D CI lower was 0.6015, and the 14D point estimate of 0.6027 suggests the CI lower would fall around 0.5930 — well below the threshold. So at 14D, both models likely fail to maintain K*=2 when accounting for uncertainty.

The real question is whether this changes the paper's main narrative. For the five models with comfortable margins, the K*=2 conclusion holds firm at 14D. For the two borderline cases, K*=2 becomes questionable. Since the paper's core claim is "7/8 configs show K*=2" at 15D, dropping to "5/8 configs" at 14D still represents a strong dominant pattern, though the borderline models introduce some fragility that the paper doesn't fully address due to missing bootstrap confidence intervals.

Looking at feature importance from other experiments, self_BLEU doesn't rank in the top features anyway—surpassing metrics like surprisal and type-token ratio dominate instead—so its removal causes minimal degradation to the overall model performance.

My assessment boils down to: K* stays at 2 across all configurations, the mean change is negligible, and while bootstrap confidence intervals are missing for a couple of the models (which could potentially cross zero), the core finding holds—self_BLEU isn't driving the K*=2 result, so it's safe to remove from the paper's perspective. I'll flag this as an info-level review since the conclusion is sound.
```

```text
exp_wiki_olmo_v2 results:
- 0v1: AUC = 0.9932 (very high, real vs gen-1 easily distinguishable)
- 1v2: AUC = 0.4986 ≈ CHANCE (below 0.50 even!)
- 2v3: AUC = 0.5012 ≈ chance
- 3v4: AUC = 0.5057 ≈ chance


- 4v5: AUC = 0.5009 ≈ chance

So K* = 1 here too, with only the first generation distinguishable from real text. The JSD confirms this pattern — massive divergence between real and gen-1 at 0.169, then it collapses to near-zero at 0.003 for subsequent generations. This matches what I saw with OLMo on arXiv, but now I'm wondering how this compares to the C4 results from earlier.

Looking back at the C4 experiment, OLMo actually achieved K* = 2 with a JSD of 0.6145 between generations 1 and 2, meaning it could sustain distinguishable signal across multiple depths. The Wikipedia results are less catastrophic than arXiv — the initial divergence is 0.169 versus 0.329 — but both still collapse to K* = 1. It seems OLMo can maintain multi-depth signal on web text like C4, but loses it entirely on academic and encyclopedia sources.

What's striking is that Qwen managed K* = 2 on Wikipedia with a 1v2 JSD of 0.6193, so the domain itself isn't the limiting factor. This points to a model-specific weakness in OLMo: it appears to only preserve depth-distinguishing structure on its primary training distribution, while other models can generalize that capability across domains.

Looking at the full picture now — C4 shows consistent K* = 2 across models (except Mistral at K* = 1), but Wikipedia and arXiv reveal a clear hierarchy where OLMo drops to K* = 1 while stronger models maintain higher K* values. I should report this pattern now rather than wait for the Pythia Wikipedia results, since it already demonstrates a robust finding about OLMo's domain-dependent limitations.

Before finalizing though, I'm verifying this isn't a data contamination issue — the experiment used a separate data directory with different initialization and produced distinct metrics (0.9932 AUC versus the earlier 0.9961, plus different feature importance rankings), so this is genuinely OLMo's behavior on Wikipedia, not an artifact. Now I need to examine the actual generation samples to see whether OLMo exhibits the same catastrophic domain failure on Wikipedia that we saw with arXiv.

These factors vary with atmospheric composition, geographic location, and time (see position of the Sun). - 2v3: AUC = 0.5445


- 3v4: AUC = 0.5205
- 4v5: AUC = 0.5168

So K* = 1 here too, with only the first generation crossing the threshold. The Jensen-Shannon divergence for 0v1 is 0.080, dramatically lower than OLMo's 0.169. What's striking is that Pythia's 1v2 AUC of 0.5846 shows some lingering depth signal compared to OLMo's 0.4986—Pythia hasn't completely flattened out after the first generation like OLMo has.

Looking at the ordinal accuracy, Pythia achieves 0.350 versus OLMo's 0.323, suggesting Pythia preserves more structural information about depth across generations. The key question is whether Pythia's lower JSD means it's staying closer to the Wikipedia distribution while genuinely losing depth signal, or if it's failing in a different way. I should examine the actual generation quality to distinguish between a true K*=1 case and an off-domain failure like OLMo's.

If the JSD sits in the normal range for C4 experiments, that would indicate Pythia's Wikipedia performance is domain-appropriate rather than a capacity collapse—which is actually more problematic for the narrative since it suggests genuine domain-specific behavior rather than a dismissible model limitation. The pattern emerging is interesting: Qwen achieves K*=2 on Wikipedia and Pythia reaches K*=2 on C4, but Pythia fails to reach K*=2 on Wikipedia despite having the capacity elsewhere.

Looking at the full results across domains, I'm seeing a complex interaction between model and dataset. Wikipedia shows Qwen at K*=2, while both Pythia and OLMo plateau at K*=1. C4 is dominated by K*=2 models except for Mistral. ArXiv shows higher K* values across the board, with OLMo again showing capacity constraints. Pythia's borderline 1v2 score on Wikipedia suggests it's right at the threshold—small changes in conditions might push it either direction.

What's striking is that Pythia's JSD on Wikipedia is actually quite normal at 0.080, similar to what I'd expect from web text. This means it's generating domain-appropriate content, not failing due to domain mismatch. The K*=1 result here is genuinely borderline, not a capacity failure like OLMo's. So the paper's framing of domain-dependent K* needs refinement—it's not just about which domain a model sees, but about how close different models sit to the decision threshold. Model size does seem to play a role—Qwen at 1.5B outperforms Pythia at 1.4B, which in turn outperforms OLMo at 1B—but the Wikipedia results don't show the clean domain hierarchy I initially thought. The core finding that K* is bounded and varies by model-domain combination actually becomes more interesting with this complexity, though it means I need to revise the paper's framing around arXiv universally giving K*=3 and Wikipedia giving K*=2, since those patterns only hold for some models. This is important enough to flag to the Director since it affects how we present the results, though it doesn't invalidate the main paper.

This is very different from OLMo! Pythia's Wikipedia continuations are:

It continues in an encyclopedic style discussing social hierarchies, which is topically related to the anarchism article. The writing quality is slightly generic/repetitive but clearly in Wikipedia style.

- Doc 1 (continuing "Albedo" article): "as it is not constant in space and time). Surface albedo depends also on the spectral distribution..." — This IS a continuation of the albedo topic! It mentions surface albedo, solar flux, atmosphere, ocean. 

The model even includes proper Wikipedia formatting like section headers and reference sections, though the content mixes some facts oddly. So Pythia is genuinely generating domain-appropriate Wikipedia text, not off-topic content like OLMo did. The K*=1 signal on Wikipedia appears to be a real depth finding rather than just a capacity limitation. After the first generation pass though, the depth-1 and depth-2 outputs become nearly indistinguishable, suggesting the model exhausts its meaningful distinctions quickly.

This is similar to what I'm seeing with Mistral on C4 — a genuine one-wash effect where a single generation pass is enough to flatten the depth structure. The key insight is that OLMo's Wikipedia K*=1 was a capacity failure while Pythia's is a legitimate one-wash phenomenon. This suggests Wikipedia as a domain actually has weaker depth signal preservation than C4 for some models. The domain hierarchy isn't as straightforward as I initially thought — arXiv maintains strong depth signals through its structured formatting, C4 shows moderate structure with most models hitting K*=2, but Wikipedia's moderate-to-low structure means even capable models like Qwen lose depth signal faster.

Looking at the full picture across domains and models, C4 shows K*=2 as the dominant pattern with Mistral as the outlier at K*=1, while Wikipedia splits between Qwen at K*=2 and both Pythia and OLMo at K*=1 — though for different reasons. The arXiv results are still coming together, but the pattern suggests K* depends on both the model's capacity and the domain's inherent structural features. The uniformity of Wikipedia's encyclopedic style might actually work against depth preservation compared to C4's diversity of web content types.

  "text": "[severity: warning] exp_wiki_pythia_v2: Pythia Wikipedia K*=1 — 这次是 genuine one-wash，非能力失败\n\n## 结果\n- 1v2 AUC = 0.5846（< θ=0.60，但显著高于 OLMo 的 0.4986）\n- 0v1 JSD = 0.080（正常范围，确认生成在域内）\n- K* = 1\n\n## 生成质量：域内、连贯\n与 OLMo 截然不同！Pythia 的 Wikipedia depth_1 样本：\n- Doc 0（续写 Anarchism 文章）: \"city-states over millennia, many of which created formal institutions to govern them...\" — 百科全书风格，主题相关\n- Doc 1（续写 Albedo 文章）: \"Surface albedo depends also on the spectral distribution of incident solar radiation...\" — 继续讨论 albedo 主题，含 \"See also\" / \"References\" 格式\n\n**这是 genuine K*=1（类似 Mistral 在 C4 的表现），不是 OLMo 的 capacity failure。** Pythia 能生成域内文本，但一代后 depth signal 就低于检测阈值。\n\n## 对论文 framing 的影响\n\n### Wikipedia 域全景\n| Model | K* | 1v2 AUC | 0v1 JSD | 机制 |\n|-------|-----|---------|---------|------|\n| Qwen-1.5B-base | 2 | 0.6193 | ~0.08 | domain-appropriate, K*=2 |\n| Pythia-1.4B | 1 | 0.5846 | 0.080 | **genuine one-wash**（域内但 signal 不足） |\n| OLMo-1B | 1 | 0.4986 | 0.169 | capacity failure（出域） |\n\n### 核心叙事冲击\n\n1. **Wikipedia 不能简单标注为 \"K*=2 domain\"**——只有 1/3 模型给出 K*=2\n2. **C1(b) \"domain-dependent\" narrative 需要修订**：K* 不仅依赖 domain，还依赖 model 的域内生成质量\n3. **但 K*≤3 bounded saturation + \"domain CAN extend K*\" 仍然成立**\n\n### 域信号强度排序（初步）\n- arXiv：结构化特征最多 → 对有能力的模型 K*=3\n- C4 web：信号中等 → 多数模型 K*=2（7/8）\n- Wikipedia：信号弱于 C4 → 仅部分模型 K*=2（1/3 目前）\n\nWikipedia 可能因风格均一（全是百科条目）导致可区分的 depth-varying features 较少；C4 更多样（新闻、博客、论坛等），不同深度的退化模式更容易被捕捉。\n\n## 关键等待\nexp_arXiv_llama8b 是决定 arXiv 叙事走向的最后一个关键实验。如果 LLaMA-8B arXiv K*=3，则 \"arXiv K*=3 for domain-competent models\" 仍可维持（3/4 排除 OLMo capacity failure）。",
  "project": "gen-depth-contamination"
}

### Assistant

_source line 420 · 2026-05-17T13:59:59.940Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent! LLaMA-3.1-8B arXiv K*=3!

Results:
- 0v1: AUC = 0.9754
- 1v2: AUC = 0.8447 (very high, > 0.60 ✓)
- 2v3: AUC = 0.6215 (> 0.60 ✓, this is the key pair)


- 3v4: AUC = 0.5362 (< 0.60 ✗)
- 4v5: AUC = 0.5331

So K* = 3 is confirmed. Now comparing this against the other small models on arXiv—Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B both also hit K*=3, but their 2v3 AUC scores are slightly higher at 0.6427 and 0.6544 respectively, whereas LLaMA-3.1-8B comes in at 0.6215.

OLMo-1B is the outlier here with K*=1, which aligns with its known capacity issues. So three out of four models converge on K*=3, which is a pretty strong pattern.

Looking at the fold stability for the 2v3 split across the five folds, I'm seeing one fold dip below 0.60 at 0.5969, which means the confidence interval probably grazes that threshold. The mean of 0.6215 stays above it though, and four out of five folds are solid.

The Jensen-Shannon divergence shows exactly what I'd expect: a clean degradation pattern starting at 0.130 for the first generation gap, dropping to 0.079 between generations one and two, then tapering to 0.018 and finally 0.008 as the signal weakens. This confirms LLaMA-8B is generating authentic arXiv text that decays predictably across iterations.

This validates the core finding—K*=3 holds up across three independent models with different architectures and scales, which is the strongest evidence the paper could ask for.
```

</details>

  "text": "[severity: info] exp_arXiv_llama8b: LLaMA-3.1-8B arXiv **K*=3 确认** — C1(b) 可升级为三模型交叉验证\n\n## 结果\n- 0v1: 0.9754, 1v2: **0.8447**, 2v3: **0.6215** (> θ=0.60 ✓), 3v4: 0.5362, 4v5: 0.5331\n- K* = 3\n- JSD 0v1=0.130（正常范围，域内生成）\n- Ordinal accuracy = 0.4549（全项目最高，arXiv depth signal 强）\n\n## 交叉验证结果\n\n| Model | Scale | K* | 2v3 AUC | 生成质量 |\n|-------|-------|-----|---------|----------|\n| Qwen-1.5B | 1.5B | 3 | 0.6427 | domain-appropriate |\n| Pythia-1.4B | 1.4B | 3 | 0.6544 | domain-appropriate |\n| **LLaMA-3.1-8B** | **8B** | **3** | **0.6215** | domain-appropriate |\n| OLMo-1B | 1B | 1 | 0.5034 | **capacity failure (off-domain)** |\n\n**3/4 模型 K*=3，唯一例外 OLMo-1B 已证实是 capacity failure（生成完全出域文本）。**\n\n## 对论文的影响\n\nC1(b) 可从 \"2 model cross-validated\" 升级为 **\"3 independent architectures (1.5B-8B) cross-validated\"**：\n- 新增 LLaMA-3.1-8B 排除了 \"small model capacity artifact\" 的顾虑（Reviewer Concern #4）\n- 跨越 1.5B→8B 的 6× scale range\n- OLMo 例外作为 \"domain competence prerequisite\" 分析放 Appendix\n\n## 注意点\nLLaMA 2v3 fold 不完全稳定：folds=[0.5969, 0.633, 0.6165, 0.6512, 0.6101]，最低 fold=0.5969 < θ。Bootstrap CI lower 可能接近 0.60 边界。论文中措辞建议：\"K*=3 with the boundary pair (2v3) AUC of 0.62\" 而非 \"robustly K*=3\"。\n\n## 当前等待实验\n- exp_wiki_llama8b (cuda:2, RUNNING): LLaMA-8B Wikipedia，测试 8B scale 能否在 Wikipedia 维持 K*=2\n- exp_arXiv_qwen7b (QUEUED): Qwen-7B arXiv，跨 scale 验证",

_source line 477 · 2026-05-17T15:29:42.267Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The Director is asking me to evaluate two things:

1. Whether including Mistral K*=1 in the JSD decay figure (with visual differentiation) is the right approach
2. Two narrative scenarios for 13B+ results (K*=1 vs K*=2)

Let me think through both carefully.

**1. Mistral in the JSD decay figure**

Looking at the current figure (fig2_auc_decay.png), it shows 4 models (Qwen-rewrite, Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-cont) on C4 web text. 

The case for adding Mistral as a dashed line with annotation is compelling—it demonstrates the full spectrum of K* behaviors and avoids the appearance of cherry-picking, especially since the paper already discusses Mistral's K*=1 pattern. However, adding Mistral opens the question of whether other models like Qwen-base, LLaMA, and Qwen-7B should also be included, which could clutter the figure. question of why only 5 of 8 models are shown
- Actually, re-reading the figure: it shows Qwen-rewrite, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen-cont. That's only 4 of the 8 web text configs. Missing: LLaMA-8B, Mistral-7B, Qwen-base, Qwen-7B.

If the figure is meant to be a representative selection, then adding Mistral as the K*=1 example makes sense. But if it should be comprehensive, all 8 should be there (which might be too cluttered).

The dashed-line approach for Mistral works well because it visually distinguishes the K*=1 case while avoiding clutter. A better alternative might be using a shaded band to show the range of AUC values across all K*=2 models, paired with a single dashed line for Mistral—this would be more efficient and demonstrate how consistent K*=2 performance is across different architectures.

Now looking at the 13B+ scenarios, the director's framing of "scale-dependent saturation acceleration" is scientifically sound—larger models do produce more human-like text, so one generation pass could plausibly approach human quality. But this creates tension with the cross-scorer validation story, since using a small model like Qwen-2.5-1.5B as the scorer might not reliably detect differences in larger model outputs.

The cross-scorer experiments showed K* is driven by the generation process, not the scorer, but those only tested generators up to 8B. At 14-32B scale, the generator might simply be too sophisticated for the 1.5B scorer to distinguish subtle depth variations. This is a methodological concern that needs addressing if K*=1 actually occurs at larger scales. The "one-wash laundering becomes the norm at scale" framing is compelling, but it requires acknowledging this scorer-capacity limitation.

Scenario B with K*=2 across the full 1B-32B range is simpler and more compelling—it's a clean universality claim that directly addresses the reviewer's concern and aligns with DPI theory. Scenario A is more interesting conceptually but carries the risk of confounding scorer limitations with genuine indistinguishability, and it requires more nuanced framing about where the effect actually holds. the effect is text-intrinsic. For 14B+ generators with K*=1, I'd need cross-scorer validation using a stronger model to rule out scorer-capacity limitations—otherwise the claim about large-scale K*=1 is confounded with the scorer's resolution. The Director's framing doesn't address this concern.

On the JSD decay figure, it's actually showing AUC decay despite the filename suggesting otherwise. The visualization is solid, and including Mistral as a dashed line with a different marker makes sense. The K*=2 shaded region annotation works well, and the θ=0.60 reference line is helpful.

Now I'm considering whether to add the new C4 experiments (LLaMA-8B, Qwen-7B, Qwen-base, and Mistral) to the figure. If I'm including Mistral, consistency suggests adding the other new models too. I could show all eight C4 configurations with K*=2 models as solid lines and Mistral as dashed, or use a spaghetti plot with a highlighted mean to demonstrate both the consistency across runs and the overall trend.
```

Mistral K*=1 在 AUC decay 图中的处理\n\n**Director 方案（虚线 + 注释）：合理，我同意。**\n\n补充建议：\n\n**当前图的问题**：只展示 4/8 C4 configs（Qwen-rw, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen-cont）。如果加 Mistral 到 5/8，审稿人仍可能问\"另外 3 个模型呢？\" 建议：\n\n- **主图**：7 条 K*=2 实线（可用 2-3 种色系区分 architecture families，低 opacity 避免视觉拥挤）+ Mistral 虚线。直观展示 \"7/8 一致性 + 1 例外\"\n- **或更简洁**：K*=2 模型用 shaded band（显示 7 条线的 min-max range）+ 单独 Mistral 虚线 + 单独 mean 实线。信息密度更高\n\n无论哪种方式，Mistral 的注释建议改为更精确的：**\"Mistral-7B (K*=1, cross-scorer validated)\"** 而非 \"text-intrinsic one-wash\"。后者是解释，不应出现在图注中。\n\n---\n\n## 2. 13B+ 叙事预案\n\n### 情况 A（K*=1）：方向正确，但有一个未被考虑的 confound\n\n**Director 的 framing 我基本同意**，\"scale-dependent saturation acceleration\" 是可辩护的叙事。但有一个关键方法学问题：\n\n**Scorer capacity confound**：当前 scorer 是 Qwen-2.5-1.5B（1.5B 参数）。如果 generator 是 14B/32B：\n- 14-32B 生成的文本质量可能超出 1.5B scorer 的 resolution\n- 1.5B 模型计算的 surprisal features 可能无法捕捉大模型输出中的 subtle depth differences\n- 此时 K*=1 可能反映的是 **scorer 的能力上限**，而非 text-intrinsic 的 one-wash\n\n**与 Mistral K*=1 的关键区别**：\n- Mistral K*=1 有 cross-scorer validation（exp-022，11/12 combos 确认）→ 确认是 text-intrinsic\n- 14B+ K*=1 **没有** cross-scorer validation → 无法排除 scorer 太弱\n- 如果声称 \"one-wash at scale\" 但审稿人问 \"did you try a more capable scorer?\"，没有数据回答\n\n**建议**（如果确实 K*=1）：\n1. **诚实标注 caveat**：\"K*=1 at 14B+ scale, measured with a 1.5B scorer. Whether this reflects text-intrinsic saturation or scorer resolution limits requires further investigation with scale-matched scorers.\"\n2. **不要等同于 Mistral K*=1**——机制可能不同\n3. **如果 GPU 允许**：用 14B 模型本身做 scorer 重跑一次 pairwise AUC。如果仍然 K*=1 → 确认 text-intrinsic；如果 K*=2 → scorer capacity 是 confound\n4. C1(a) 措辞改为 \"K*=2 is the dominant mode for ≤8B models on web text; at 14B+ scale, K* may decrease to 1, though disentangling text-intrinsic saturation from scorer resolution limits requires scale-matched evaluation\"\n\n**\"One-wash laundering becomes the norm at scale\" 这个表述有风险**——除非排除 scorer confound，否则审稿人会攻击。建议降级为 \"suggestive evidence\" 而非 \"confirmed finding\"。\n\n### 情况 B（K*=2）：无异议\n\n简洁、强力、与 paper_framing.md 完全兼容。几乎不需要修改：\n- C1(a) 升级 \"7/8 at ≤8B\" → \"K*=2 across 1B-32B (20× scale range, 5+ architecture families)\"\n- 直接回应 Reviewer Concern #2\n- 无 scorer confound 问题（K*=2 说明 1.5B scorer 仍有足够 resolution）\n\n### 两种情况的论文结构影响\n\n| 维度 | 情况 A (K*=1) | 情况 B (K*=2) |\n|------|--------------|--------------|\n| C1(a) 措辞 | 需 scope 限定 + caveat | 直接升级 |\n| 新增章节 | \"Scale Dependence\" analysis (~0.5 page) | 一段 + 更新 Table |\n| Reviewer 攻击面 | scorer confound 是新攻击面 | 攻击面收窄 |\n| 论文 impact | 可能更高（surprising finding）但风险大 | 稳健增强 |\n| paper_framing.md 兼容性 | 中度修改 | 无缝升级 |\n\n---\n\n## 总结\n\n1. Mistral 图表方案合理，建议考虑展示全部 8 configs 或用 shaded band\n2. 情况 B 是 strictly better outcome（无 confound，纯加分）\n3. 情况 A 可行但**必须**先排除 scorer capacity confound（用大模型做 scorer 验证），否则 \"one-wash at scale\" claim 站不住",
  "project": "gen-depth-contamination"
}

[System 触发审查]:
请审查两项刚落地的结果及其论文整合：

**1. 语义特征消融结果审查**
Agent 报告：17D (15D + semantic_cosine_sim + entity_diversity) vs 15D，K* 不变，AUC diff 均在 ±0.005。
- 请核实：这个消融是否充分？只用了 LLaMA-8B 一个模型还是多模型？如果是单模型，claim "surface statistics suffice" 是否 overclaim？
- semantic_cosine_sim 用了什么 embedding model？如果用的 all-MiniLM-L6-v2（384D），这个模型本身是否太弱？
- 论文措辞建议：是 "semantic features do not improve K*" 还是更保守的 "tested semantic features..."？

**2. JSD decay λ 量化结果审查**
Paper Agent 已写入 Discussion。请审查 `discussion.tex` 中新增的 "JSD decay and the discrimination boundary" 段落：
- λ 的计算方法是否 rigorous？是 exponential fit 还是简单 ratio？
- "contraction rate governed by domain structure, not model architecture" 这个 claim 的数据支撑是否充分？C4 上 4 个模型 λ≈2.9 的 variance 是多少？
- λ 是否可以从 empirical observation 升级为 theoretical contribution？比如：λ 与 domain perplexity / entropy 的关系是否可量化？这能否支撑一个 proposition（不是 theorem）？

**3. Claim 强度评估**
站在审稿人角度：当前 40 个成功实验 + JSD λ 机制分析 + 语义消融 negative result，整体论文的 claim 是否还有提升空间？特别是 λ 量化是否足够把论文从 "systematic empirical study" 推向 "empirical + theoretical" 定位？

_source line 512 · 2026-05-17T16:16:50.269Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01EDx3E8Y3fHKV7pvAanCNcB`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 C4 models with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{\geq}4$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.9$--$6.3$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
19	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
20	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
21	
22	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
23	The token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two distinct decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}).
24	For C4 web text, 0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 10--50$\times$ across all 7 models with $\Kempirical{=}2$ (median cliff ratio ${\sim}$16$\times$), and subsequent pairs converge to a noise floor of ${\sim}$0.003--0.005.
25	Fitting $\jsd(k, k{+}1) \propto e^{-\lambda k}$ yields $\lambda \approx 2.9$--$6.3$ for C4, consistent with rapid mixing of $G_\delta$ toward its stationary distribution.
26	The empirical decay rate $\lambda$ is highly consistent within each domain but varies across domains: C4 yields $\lambda \approx 2.9$, Wikipedia $\lambda \approx 4.2$, and arXiv $\lambda \approx 1.0$ (excluding OLMo, an outlier with cliff ratio 47$\times$).
27	This cross-model consistency indicates that the contraction rate of $G_\delta$ is governed primarily by the structure of the input domain rather than the model architecture.
28	ArXiv text decays 3$\times$ slower than C4 ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$), with a correspondingly higher convergence floor (${\sim}$0.007--0.009 vs.\ ${\sim}$0.003--0.004).
29	Formulaic structural features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, section templates) degrade more slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth-distinguishing signal, which explains the elevated $\Kempirical{=}3$.
30	The retrain-chain exhibits a fundamentally different profile: $\lambda = 0.285$ with a cliff ratio of only 1.31$\times$ and no sharp drop between adjacent pairs.
31	This gradual, sustained divergence reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts rather than text-intrinsic information loss, further supporting the distinction between prompt-chain and retrain-chain $\Kempirical$ (Section~\ref{sec:boundary_cases}).
32	
33	\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}
34	For web text, 2v3 $\auc < \theta$ across all model configurations, so depth assignments for $d \geq 2$ approximate random labels; graduated weighting injects noise, explaining convergence toward binary at high $\alpha$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
35	DSIR fails differently: bigram hashing captures domain shift but not depth-correlated signatures.
36	This pattern persists across contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}), indicating the failure is structural rather than severity-dependent.
37	
38	\paragraph{Representation dependence.}
39	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia and OLMo match the 15D boundary at $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).
40	The 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (7/8 configurations $\Kempirical{\leq}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.
41	Cross-scorer validation is consistent with $\Kempirical$ being governed by the generation operator rather than the scoring model ($\rho > 0.90$ feature ranking preservation; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), though $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}}{=}3$ for Qwen indicates that representation choice can modulate the boundary.
42	
43	\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$.}
44	Web text (C4, Wikipedia) consistently yields $\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent model families (Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B; Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
45	Domain-specific stylistic constraints amplify per-generation distributional shift, making contamination in structured corpora trackable to greater depth.
46	
47	\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain $\Kempirical$.}
48	Retrain-chain experiments (Pythia-1.4B LoRA) yield $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.9484$, 2v3 $= 0.8888$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$; bootstrap CIs in Table~\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary of $\Kempirical{=}2$.
49	The elevated retrain-chain signal arises from cumulative model-adaptation artifacts (vocabulary narrowing, repetition bias) that inject additional discriminative signal beyond text-intrinsic information loss.
50	These two boundaries address distinct operational questions.
51	Prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ measures text-level information decay: after one generation pass, the text itself carries insufficient signal to distinguish further recycling, regardless of how many times it has been regenerated.
52	This is the relevant boundary for data curation on wild web text, where one cannot assume that synthetic documents originate from a retrain pipeline.
53	Retrain-chain $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ reflects model-adaptation artifacts that accumulate across fine-tuning iterations (setup-specific vocabulary narrowing, increased repetition); these artifacts are detectable but do not imply proportionally greater text quality degradation.
54	The two protocols are complementary rather than contradictory: prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ provides the operational lower bound for corpus filtering, while retrain-chain $\Kempirical$ characterizes model-level drift in closed-loop training scenarios.
55	
56	\paragraph{Boundary cases and scale effect.}
57	Of 8 tested model configurations on C4, 7 yield $\Kempirical{=}2$; Mistral-7B is the sole sub-2 exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$; 1v2 $\auc = 0.594$, CI $[0.584, 0.603]$), validating $\theta{=}0.60$ as an effective discriminator (gap: $0.594$ vs.\ $0.611$--$0.696$).

Blue band: min-max range across 7 C4 models with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$). 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nThe token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two distinct decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}).\nFor C4 web text, 0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 10--50$\\times$ across all 7 models with $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (median cliff ratio ${\\sim}$16$\\times$), and subsequent pairs converge to a noise floor of ${\\sim}$0.003--0.005.\nFitting $\\jsd(k, k{+}1) \\propto e^{-\\lambda k}$ yields $\\lambda \\approx 2.9$--$6.3$ for C4, consistent with rapid mixing of $G_\\delta$ toward its stationary distribution.\nThe empirical decay rate $\\lambda$ is highly consistent within each domain but varies across domains: C4 yields $\\lambda \\approx 2.9$, Wikipedia $\\lambda \\approx 4.2$, and arXiv $\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ (excluding OLMo, an outlier with cliff ratio 47$\\times$).\nThis cross-model consistency indicates that the contraction rate of $G_\\delta$ is governed primarily by the structure of the input domain rather than the model architecture.\nArXiv text decays 3$\\times$ slower than C4 ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$), with a correspondingly higher convergence floor (${\\sim}$0.007--0.009 vs.\\ ${\\sim}$0.003--0.004).\nFormulaic structural features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, section templates) degrade more slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth-distinguishing signal, which explains the elevated $\\Kempirical{=}3$.\nThe retrain-chain exhibits a fundamentally different profile: $\\lambda = 0.285$ with a cliff ratio of only 1.31$\\times$ and no sharp drop between adjacent pairs.\nThis gradual, sustained divergence reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts rather than text-intrinsic information loss, further supporting the distinction between prompt-chain and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ (Section~\\ref{sec:boundary_cases}).\n\n\\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}\nFor web text, 2v3 $\\auc < \\theta$ across all model configurations, so depth assignments for $d \\geq 2$ approximate random labels; graduated weighting injects noise, explaining convergence toward binary at high $\\alpha$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nDSIR fails differently: bigram hashing captures domain shift but not depth-correlated signatures.\nThis pattern persists across contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}), indicating the failure is structural rather than severity-dependent.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia and OLMo match the 15D boundary at $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (7/8 configurations $\\Kempirical{\\leq}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nCross-scorer validation is consistent with $\\Kempirical$ being governed by the generation operator rather than the scoring model ($\\rho > 0.90$ feature ranking preservation; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), though $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}}{=}3$ for Qwen indicates that representation choice can modulate the boundary.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) consistently yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent model families (Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\nDomain-specific stylistic constraints amplify per-generation distributional shift, making contamination in structured corpora trackable to greater depth.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$.}\nRetrain-chain experiments (Pythia-1.4B LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.9484$, 2v3 $= 0.8888$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$; bootstrap CIs in Table~\\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary of $\\Kempirical{=}2$.\nThe elevated retrain-chain signal arises from cumulative model-adaptation artifacts (vocabulary narrowing, repetition bias) that inject additional discriminative signal beyond text-intrinsic information loss.\nThese two boundaries address distinct operational questions.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ measures text-level information decay: after one generation pass, the text itself carries insufficient signal to distinguish further recycling, regardless of how many times it has been regenerated.\nThis is the relevant boundary for data curation on wild web text, where one cannot assume that synthetic documents originate from a retrain pipeline.\nRetrain-chain $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ reflects model-adaptation artifacts that accumulate across fine-tuning iterations (setup-specific vocabulary narrowing, increased repetition); these artifacts are detectable but do not imply proportionally greater text quality degradation.\nThe two protocols are complementary ```text
完整结果如下：

---

## Semantic Feature Ablation 完整结果

**数据**: exp_018_llama8b (C4 语料, 5000 docs/depth, depths 0-5)  
**模型**: Qwen2.5-1.5B (用于生成 surprisal 特征的 LLM)  
**语义特征方法**: TF-IDF cosine similarity (相邻句子间) + regex entity diversity (大写多词短语) —— **未使用外部 embedding 模型**，是纯 TF-IDF 向量化

### Per-pair AUC 对比

| Depth Pair | 15D AUC | 17D AUC | Diff |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0v1 | 0.9225 | 0.9232 | +0.0007 |
| 1v2 | 0.6123 | 0.6099 | -0.0024 |
| 2v3 | 0.5274 | 0.5239 | -0.0035 |
| 3v4 | 0.5169 | 0.5201 | +0.0032 |
| 4v5 | 0.5162 | 0.5120 | -0.0042 |

**K\* = 2**（15D 和 17D 均不变）

### 17D 全特征重要性排名

| Rank | Feature | Importance |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | surp_d1_skew | — |
| 2 | freq_entropy | — |
| 3 | surp_mean | — |
| 4 | surp_std | — |
| 5 | surp_d2_mean | — |
| 6 | surp_d1_mean | — |
| 7 | freq_kurtosis | — |
| 8 | surp_kurt | — |
| 9 | surp_skew | — |
| 10 | surp_d1_std | — |
| **11** | **semantic_cosine_sim** | **277.8** |
| 12 | hapax_ratio | — |
| 13 | ttr | — |
| 14 | low_freq_ratio | — |
| 15 | surp_d2_std | — |
| 16 | self_bleu | — |
| **17** | **entity_diversity** | **76.8 (最末)** |

### 关键参数

- 分类器: LightGBM (200 estimators, max_depth=6, lr=0.05, num_leaves=31)
- 验证: GroupKFold (按 doc_id 分组，5 fold)
- 15D 特征: surprisal 统计 (mean/std/skew/kurt + 1st/2nd derivatives) + 词汇多样性 (ttr, hapax_ratio, self_bleu, freq_kurtosis, freq_entropy, low_freq_ratio)
- 新增 2D: semantic_cosine_sim (TF-IDF, max_features=10000, sublinear_tf), entity_diversity (regex capitalized phrases unique/total)

### 结论

语义特征对 depth 判别无贡献。两项语义特征排名 11/17 和 17/17，K\* 不变，各 depth pair 的 AUC 差异在 ±0.004 以内（噪声级）。Surface statistical features 足以捕捉所有可判别的 depth 信号。

**注意**: 这里的"semantic"用的是 TF-IDF 而非 dense embedding model，所以严格来说测试的是浅层语义相似性，不是深层语义表征。

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the Research Reviewer (Loop #15) for project "gen-depth-contamination" targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR (deadline May 25, 2026). My role is independent scientific verification of experiment conclusions, design quality, and paper claims. I communicate exclusively via `reviewer_post_to_director`. The most recent request asks me to review: (a) semantic feature ablation (17D vs 15D) sufficiency, (b) JSD decay λ quantification in discussion.tex, and (c) overall claim strength assessment.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **K* (discrimination boundary)**: Maximum depth k where pairwise AUC(k-1 vs k) > θ=0.60. Core finding: K*∈{1,2,3}, domain-dependent bounded saturation.
   - **15D model-free features**: surprisal statistics (mean/std/skew/kurt + 1st/2nd derivatives) + lexical diversity (ttr, hapax_ratio, self_bleu, freq_kurtosis, freq_entropy, low_freq_ratio). Used with LightGBM classifier.
   - **Domain dependence**: C4 web text K*=2 (7/8 models), arXiv K*=3 (3/4 models cross-validated), Wikipedia mixed (1/3 K*=2)
   - **One-wash laundering**: Single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content
   - **Scorer**: Qwen-2.5-1.5B used for surprisal feature extraction across all experiments
   - **pp unit**: 1pp = 0.01 (must double-check before reporting; historical 10× errors)
   - **JSD decay λ**: Exponential fit JSD(k,k+1) ∝ e^{-λk}. C4 λ≈2.9, Wikipedia λ≈4.2, arXiv λ≈1.0, retrain-chain λ=0.285
   - **C3 claim**: Binary K*-aware filtering outperforms graduated filtering (graduated ≈ no_filter)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **paper_framing.md** — Core paper claims (C1, C3), anticipated reviewer concerns, story spine. Read to assess coverage gaps and framing decisions.
   - **report.md** — Current experiment status snapshot. Shows 39 success / 9 failed / 0 method-negative (total ~59 experiments now).
   - **ROADMAP.md** — Research dashboard. Reviewed and found coherent. exp_008_retrain_chain stuck in PREPARING.
   - **artifacts/theta_sensitivity_table.csv** — K* values for 11 model-configs across θ=[0.55, 0.575, 0.60, 0.625, 0.65, 0.70]. Found caption overclaims "stable" when θ=0.55 shows violations.
   - **artifacts/theta_sensitivity_table.tex** — LaTeX table for paper. Caption needs revision: "web text K*≤2" violated at θ=0.55.
   - **artifacts/gen_theta_sensitivity.py** — Script computing K* from AUC values at different thresholds. Logic verified correct.
   - **artifacts/exp_B_14d_ablation.json** — 14D vs 15D ablation results for 4 models (Qwen-rw, Pythia, OLMo, Qwen-cont). All K*=2 maintained, mean Δ=-0.0015. OLMo/Qwen-cont margins extremely thin (+0.34pp/+0.27pp).
   - **artifacts/fig2_auc_decay.png** — AUC decay figure showing 4 C4 models. Currently missing Mistral and 3 other models.
   - **docs/paper/discussion.tex** — Discussion section with new JSD decay paragraph (lines 22-31). Contains λ quantification, domain comparison, retrain-chain contrast. Key claim: "contraction rate governed primarily by domain structure rather than model architecture."
   - **Semantic ablation results** (no artifact file, found via registry/scripts on server): 17D vs 15D on LLaMA-8B only, TF-IDF-based semantic_cosine_sim + regex entity_diversity. Both features ranked bottom (11/17, 17/17), AUC diffs within ±0.004.

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Data reuse bug (CRITICAL)**: exp_wiki_pythia and exp_wiki_olmo used shared data directory (data/exp_023_wiki/) containing pre-existing depth_*.jsonl and features.csv from exp-023 (Qwen-base). Pipeline skipped generation, producing identical results for "different" models. Fix: Agent resubmitted as v2 with independent data directories (data/exp_wiki_pythia/, data/exp_wiki_olmo/) containing only depth_0.jsonl.
   - **14D ablation v1 worker failed**: worker_analysis_14d_ablation_bootstrap failed (status=failed). v2 succeeded but output only point estimates, missing bootstrap CIs despite worker name containing "bootstrap."
   - **exp_arXiv_olmo negative result**: OLMo-1B arXiv K*=1 due to catastrophic off-domain generation (arXiv abstracts → blog posts). Identified via generation sample inspection and JSD 0v1=0.329 (3× normal). Not a genuine K* finding but model capacity failure.
   - **exp_wiki_pythia_v2 K*=1**: Pythia-1.4B Wikipedia K*=1 with 1v2=0.5846 (borderline). Unlike OLMo, this IS genuine one-wash (domain-appropriate generation confirmed, JSD=0.080 normal). Requires narrative revision.

5. Problem Solving:

   - Distinguished two mechanisms of K*=1: capacity failure (OLMo on non-C4 domains) vs genuine one-wash (Pythia on Wikipedia, Mistral on C4)

   **1.         "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 C4 models with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$--$6.3$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nThe token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two distinct decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}).\nFor C4 web text, 0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 10--50$\\times$ across all 7 models with $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (median cliff ratio ${\\sim}$16$\\times$), and subsequent pairs converge to a noise floor of ${\\sim}$0.003--0.005.\nFitting $\\jsd(k, k{+}1) \\propto e^{-\\lambda k}$ yields $\\lambda \\approx 2.9$--$6.3$ for C4, consistent with rapid mixing of $G_\\delta$ toward its stationary distribution.\nThe empirical decay rate $\\lambda$ is highly consistent within each domain but varies across domains: C4 yields $\\lambda \\approx 2.9$, Wikipedia $\\lambda \\approx 4.2$, and arXiv $\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ (excluding OLMo, an outlier with cliff ratio 47$\\times$).\nThis cross-model consistency indicates that the contraction rate of $G_\\delta$ is governed primarily by the structure of the input domain rather than the model architecture.\nArXiv text decays 3$\\times$ slower than C4 ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$), with a correspondingly higher convergence floor (${\\sim}$0.007--0.009 vs.\\ ${\\sim}$0.003--0.004).\nFormulaic structural features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, section templates) degrade more slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth-distinguishing signal, which explains the elevated $\\Kempirical{=}3$.\nThe retrain-chain exhibits a fundamentally different profile: $\\lambda = 0.285$ with a cliff ratio of only 1.31$\\times$ and no sharp drop between adjacent pairs.\nThis gradual, sustained divergence reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts rather than text-intrinsic information loss, further supporting the distinction between prompt-chain and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ (Section~\\ref{sec:boundary_cases}).\n\n\\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}\nFor web text, 2v3 $\\auc < \\theta$ across all model configurations, so depth assignments for $d \\geq 2$ approximate random labels; graduated weighting injects noise, explaining convergence toward binary at high $\\alpha$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nDSIR fails differently: bigram hashing captures domain shift but not depth-correlated signatures.\nThis pattern persists across contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}), indicating the failure is structural rather than severity-dependent.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia and OLMo match the 15D boundary at $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (7/8 configurations $\\Kempirical{\\leq}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nCross-scorer validation is consistent with $\\Kempirical$ being governed by the generation operator rather than the scoring model ($\\rho > 0.90$ feature ranking preservation; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), though $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}}{=}3$ for Qwen indicates that representation choice can modulate the boundary.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) consistently yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across two independent model families (Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\nDomain-specific stylistic constraints amplify per-generation distributional shift, making contamination in structured corpora trackable to greater depth.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$.}\nRetrain-chain experiments (Pythia-1.4B LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.9484$, 2v3 $= 0.8888$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$; bootstrap CIs in Table~\\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary of $\\Kempirical{=}2$.\nThe elevated retrain-chain signal arises from cumulative model-adaptation artifacts (vocabulary narrowing, repetition bias) that inject additional discriminative signal beyond text-intrinsic information loss.\nThese two boundaries address distinct operational questions.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ measures text-level information decay: after one generation pass, the text itself carries insufficient signal to distinguish further recycling, regardless of how many times it has been regenerated.\nThis is the relevant boundary for data curation on wild web text, where one cannot assume that synthetic documents originate from a retrain pipeline.\nRetrain-chain $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ reflects model-adaptation artifacts that accumulate across fine-tuning iterations (setup-specific vocabulary narrowing, increased repetition); these artifacts are detectable but do not imply proportionally greater text quality degradation.\nThe two protocols are complementary rather than contradictory: prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ provides the operational lower bound for corpus filtering, while retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ characterizes model-level drift in closed-loop training scenarios.\n\n\\paragraph{Boundary cases and scale effect.}\nOf 8 tested model configurations on C4, 7 yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Mistral-7B is the sole sub-2 exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$; 1v2 $\\auc = 0.594$, CI $[0.584, 0.603]$), validating $\\theta{=}0.60$ as an effective discriminator (gap: $0.594$ vs.\\ $0.611$--$0.696$).\nLarger models produce more human-like text, reducing 0v1 JSD: 0.15--0.27 at $\\leq$1.5B, 0.077 at 8B (LLaMA-3.1-8B).\nThis trend suggests that models at 13B+ may increasingly yield $\\Kempirical{=}1$; this speculative prediction warrants future verification.\n",

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "0b7b42bb-4944-4d05-8f4d-15596f539e6f",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/artifacts/gen_theta_sensitivity.py",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/artifacts/gen_theta_sensitivity.py",
        "content": "import csv\nimport os\n\nOUTPUT_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\n\nconfigs = [\n    # (short_name, display_name, domain, [0v1, 1v2, 2v3, 3v4, 4v5], footnote)\n    (\"Qwen-rw\",         \"Qwen-1.5B-Inst (rewrite)\",   \"web\", [0.9956, 0.6955, 0.5893, 0.5435, 0.5340], \"\"),\n    (\"Pythia-cont\",     \"Pythia-1.4B (cont.)\",         \"web\", [0.9932, 0.6539, 0.5514, 0.5152, 0.5136], \"\"),\n    (\"OLMo-cont\",       \"OLMo-1B (cont.)\",             \"web\", [0.9970, 0.6145, 0.5496, 0.5091, 0.5045], \"\"),\n    (\"Qwen-cont\",       \"Qwen-1.5B-Inst (cont.)\",     \"web\", [0.9971, 0.6112, 0.5609, 0.5409, 0.5141], \"\"),\n    (\"LLaMA-8B\",        \"LLaMA-3.1-8B (cont.)\",       \"web\", [0.9225, 0.6123, 0.5274, 0.5138, 0.5097], \"\"),\n    (\"Mistral-7B\",      \"Mistral-7B (cont.)$^\\\\dagger$\",\"web\",[0.9843, 0.5935, 0.5402, 0.5232, 0.5181], \"†\"),\n    (\"Qwen-base\",       \"Qwen-1.5B-base (cont.)\",     \"web\", [0.9870, 0.6262, 0.5496, 0.5294, 0.5182], \"\"),\n    (\"Qwen-7B\",         \"Qwen-7B (cont.)\",             \"web\", [0.9644, 0.6178, 0.5460, 0.5225, 0.5091], \"\"),\n    (\"Qwen-rw-arXiv\",   \"Qwen-1.5B-Inst (rewrite)\",   \"arxiv\",[0.9946, 0.8580, 0.6427, 0.5437, 0.5294], \"\"),\n    (\"Pythia-arXiv\",    \"Pythia-1.4B (cont.)\",         \"arxiv\",[0.9877, 0.8047, 0.6545, 0.5507, 0.5287], \"\"),\n    (\"Mistral-7B-cross\",\"Mistral-7B (Qwen scorer)$^\\\\ddagger$\",\"cross\",[0.9209, 0.5695, 0.5335, 0.5140, 0.5184], \"‡\"),\n]\n\nthetas = [0.55, 0.575, 0.60, 0.625, 0.65, 0.70]\n\ndef compute_kstar(aucs, theta):\n    \"\"\"K* = max k such that AUC(k-1 vs k) > theta. AUC list is [0v1, 1v2, 2v3, 3v4, 4v5].\"\"\"\n    kstar = 0\n    for i, auc in enumerate(aucs):\n        k = i + 1  # transition 0v1 -> k=1, 1v2 -> k=2, etc.\n        if auc > theta:\n            kstar = k\n        else:\n            break\n    return kstar\n\n# Compute all K* values\nresults = []\nfor short, display, domain, aucs, fn in configs:\n    row = {\"short\": short, \"display\": display, \"domain\": domain, \"footnote\": fn}\n    for t in thetas:\n        row[f\"theta_{t}\"] = compute_kstar(aucs, t)\n    results.append(row)\n\n# --- CSV ---\ncsv_path = os.path.join(OUTPUT_DIR, \"theta_sensitivity_table.csv\")\nwith open(csv_path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as f:\n    writer = csv.writer(f)\n    header = [\"Model-Config\"] + [f\"theta_{t}\" for t in thetas]\n    writer.writerow(header)\n    for r in results:\n        csv_display = r[\"display\"].replace(\"$^\\\\dagger$\", \"†\").replace(\"$^\\\\ddagger$\", \"‡\")\n        writer.writerow([csv_display] + [r[f\"theta_{t}\"] for t in thetas])\nprint(f\"CSV written to {csv_path}\")\n\n# --- LaTeX ---\ntex_path = os.path.join(OUTPUT_DIR, \"theta_sensitivity_table.tex\")\n\ntheta_cols = \" \".join([\"c\"] * len(thetas))\nheader_cells = []\nfor t in thetas:\n    label = f\"{t:.3f}\" if t == 0.575 or t == 0.625 else f\"{t:.2f}\"\n    if t == 0.60:\n        header_cells.append(r\"\\textbf{$\\theta=0.60$}\")\n    else:\n        header_cells.append(f\"$\\\\theta={label}$\")\n\nlines = []\nlines.append(r\"\\begin{table}[t]\")\nlines.append(r\"\\centering\")\nlines.append(r\"\\small\")\nlines.append(r\"\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{4pt}\")\nlines.append(r\"\\caption{Sensitivity of $K^*$ to threshold $\\theta$. The qualitative pattern (web text $K^*\\!\\leq\\!2$, arXiv $K^*\\!=\\!3$, cross-scorer $K^*\\!=\\!1$) is stable across $\\theta\\in[0.55,\\,0.70]$. \\textbf{Bold column} marks $\\theta=0.60$, the default used throughout the paper. $\\dagger$\\,Self-scorer. $\\ddagger$\\,Cross-scorer (Qwen scores Mistral generations).}\")\nlines.append(r\"\\label{tab:theta_sensitivity}\")\nlines.append(r\"\\begin{tabular}{l \" + theta_cols + \"}\")\nlines.append(r\"\\toprule\")\nlines.append(\"Model-Config & \" + \" & \".join(header_cells) + r\" \\\\\")\nlines.append(r\"\\midrule\")\n\ndomain_order = [\"web\", \"arxiv\", \"cross\"]\ndomain_labels = {\"web\": \"C4 Web-text\", \"arxiv\": \"arXiv\", \"cross\": \"Cross-scorer\"}\nfirst_domain = True\n\nfor domain in domain_order:\n    domain_rows = [r for r in results if r[\"domain\"] == domain]\n    if not domain_rows:\n        continue\n    if not first_domain:\n        lines.append(r\"\\midrule\")\n    lines.append(r\"\\multicolumn{\" + str(len(thetas) + 1) + r\"}{l}{\\textit{\" + domain_labels[domain] + r\"}} \\\\\")\n    for r in domain_rows:\n        cells = []\n        for t in thetas:\n            val = r[f\"theta_{t}\"]\n            if t == 0.60:\n                cells.append(r\"\\textbf{\" + str(val) + \"}\")\n            else:\n                cells.append(str(val))\n        lines.append(f\"~~{r['display']} & \" + \" & \".join(cells) + r\" \\\\\")\n    first_domain = False\n\nlines.append(r\"\\bottomrule\")\nlines.append(r\"\\end{tabular}\")\nlines.append(r\"\\end{table}\")\n\ntex_content = \"\\n\".join(lines)\nwith open(tex_path, \"w\") as f:\n    f.write(tex_content)\nprint(f\"LaTeX written to {tex_path}\")\n\n# --- Verify ---\nprint(\"\\n=== K* Summary ===\")\nfor r in results:\n    vals = [str(r[f\"theta_{t}\"]) for t in thetas]\n    csv_display = r[\"display\"].replace(\"$^\\\\dagger$\", \"†\").replace(\"$^\\\\ddagger$\", \"‡\")\n    print(f\"  {csv_display:40s} | {' | '.join(vals)}\")\n",
        "numLines": 114,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 114
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/gen_theta_sensitivity.py"
  },
  "type": "attachment",

- **arXiv: λ ≈ 1.0** ✓ (actual mean = 0.99, 3x slower than C4)
- **retrain-chain: λ = 0.285** ✓ (actual = 0.2851)

```text
Perfect! I now have all the information needed. Let me compile the final report.

## Summary of Semantic Feature Ablation Results

I've successfully located and reviewed the semantic feature ablation results for the gen-depth-contamination project. Here are the key findings:

### Primary Result Files

1. **Semantic Ablation Report**  
   `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/artifacts/_project/report/semantic_ablation_report.md`

2. **Worker Analysis Result**  
   `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/logs/workers/worker_analysis_semantic_feature_ablation_20260517T151744_result.json`

3. **Worker Logs**  
   `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/logs/workers/worker_analysis_semantic_feature_ablation_20260517T151744.log` (356KB)  
   `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/logs/workers/worker_paper_semantic_ablation_table_20260517T161724.log` (111KB)

### Key Semantic Ablation Results (15D vs 17D Features)

**Feature Configuration:**
- **15D baseline**: surprisal stats (mean, std, skew, kurtosis), delta-surprisal stats, TTR, hapax ratio, self-BLEU, frequency-based features (kurtosis, entropy, low-freq ratio)
- **17D expanded**: 15D + `semantic_cosine_sim` + `entity_diversity`

**AUC Impact (C4, Qwen2.5-1.5B, continuation mode):**

| Depth Pair | 15D AUC | 17D AUC | Delta   |
|-----------|---------|---------|---------|
| 0v1       | 0.9225  | 0.9232  | +0.0007 |
| 1v2       | 0.6123  | 0.6099  | -0.0024 |
| 2v3       | 0.5274  | 0.5239  | -0.0035 |
| 3v4       | 0.5169  | 0.5201  | +0.0032 |
| 4v5       | 0.5162  | 0.5120  | -0.0042 |

**Key Finding: K* remains unchanged at 2 for both 15D and 17D models**

### Feature Importance Rankings (17D Model)

- **semantic_cosine_sim**: Rank 11/17 (importance score = 277.8)
- **entity_diversity**: Rank 17/17 (importance score = 76.8) — **dead last**

### Conclusion

The semantic features (TF-IDF cosine similarity and entity diversity) add **no discriminative power** to the depth boundary determination. AUC differences are all within ±0.005 noise range. The two new semantic features rank in the bottom half of feature importance (11th and 17th out of 17), indicating that **surface statistical features (surprisal statistics, frequency entropy) dominate the classification**, not semantic content similarity.

This finding validates the choice of model-free 15D features for the paper's core K* characterization, and explains why semantic features were not included in the final feature set despite initial exploration.
```

If OLMo on C4 exists and has a high λ value around 6.3, that would explain the discrepancy—but I need to verify whether that additional data actually exists or if there's a mismatch in what was discovered. I'm identifying three key issues to flag: the semantic ablation relies on a limited setup that may not fully support the paper's claims about surface statistics, the Wikipedia λ value appears inconsistent depending on whether OLMo is excluded, and the C4 range seems inflated compared to the actual data points available.

The exponential fits lack statistical rigor—no R² values or confidence intervals despite having only 4-5 data points per curve, which raises overfitting concerns. Overall, the paper works better as an empirical study with mechanistic insights rather than a theoretical contribution, since the λ analysis, while valuable, doesn't have formal grounding connecting domain properties to the observed decay rates.
```

JSD decay λ 量化 [severity: warning]\n\n**数据核实**（来自 jsd_decay_analysis.json）：\n- C4 实际 λ 值：LLaMA-8B=2.15, Qwen-1.5B=3.16, Mistral-7B=3.51, Pythia-1.4B=2.88。均值=2.93，范围 2.15-3.51。\n- Wikipedia 实际 λ 值（排除 OLMo）：Qwen-1.5B=2.98, Pythia-1.4B=3.31。均值=3.14。含 OLMo(λ=6.28) 均值=4.19。\n- arXiv 实际 λ 值（排除 OLMo）：LLaMA-8B=0.75, Qwen-7B=1.03, Pythia-1.4B=1.19。均值=0.99。\n- Retrain-chain：λ=0.285。✓\n\n**问题 A — Wikipedia λ≈4.2 数据不一致**：discussion.tex 第 26 行写 \"Wikipedia λ ≈ 4.2\"，同时括号注 \"(excluding OLMo, an outlier with cliff ratio 47×)\"。但排除 OLMo 后 Wikipedia 实际 λ=3.14；只有包含 OLMo(λ=6.28) 才得到 4.2。句子结构使读者以为三个域均排除了 OLMo，但 Wikipedia 值却包含了 OLMo。这是数据报告错误或歧义。\n\n**问题 B — C4 λ≈2.9--6.3 上界来源不明**：discussion.tex 第 25 行 \"yields λ ≈ 2.9--6.3 for C4\"。4 个已验证 C4 模型范围为 2.15-3.51。6.3 似来自 OLMo Wikipedia(6.28) 或跨域汇总，不属于 C4。若有额外 C4 模型 JSD 数据（图 caption 声称 7 个），需验证是否真有 λ>6 的 C4 模型。\n\n**问题 C — 拟合严谨性**：每条曲线仅 4-5 个数据点（depths 0-4），无 R²/CI 报告。指数拟合 3 参数（a, λ, c）在 4 点上过拟合风险高。建议至少报告 R² 或残差信息，或在论文中注明\"qualitative characterization\"而非暗示精确估计。\n\n**建议**：(1) 统一 OLMo 排除规则，Wikipedia 值应改为 ≈3.1 或明确说明含 OLMo；(2) 核实 \"2.9-6.3 for C4\" 的 7 模型数据源；(3) 补充 fit quality 指标或降低精度声称。\n\n---\n\n### 3. Claim 强度评估 [severity: info]\n\n**现状**：40+ 实验覆盖 8 C4 模型 + 3 arXiv + 3 Wikipedia + retrain-chain + 3 baselines + θ sensitivity + neural probe。JSD λ 分析提供了 K* 的信息论机制解释（信息收缩速率 → 域结构而非模型架构决定）。\n\n**评估**：\n- λ 分析将论文从纯 empirical 提升为 \"empirical with mechanistic insight\"，这对 EMNLP 来说是加分项\n- 但**不构成理论贡献**：没有从域属性（如词汇多样性、结构约束）推导 λ 的形式化证明\n- \"domain structure > model architecture\" 这个 claim 的统计支撑：C4 域内 CV≈0.17，arXiv 域内 CV≈0.22，两域不重叠（C4: 2.15-3.51 vs arXiv: 0.75-1.19），方向正确但样本量有限（n=3-4 per domain）\n- 论文 framing 应定位为 **\"large-scale empirical characterization + mechanistic explanation\"** 而非理论贡献\n\n**整体判断**：实验基础扎实，λ 分析是有价值的补充。主要风险点在于 §2 中的数值报告错误（Wikipedia λ 和 C4 λ range），若不修正将给审稿人留下数据不严谨的印象。语义消融的单模型限制不是致命问题但需要在措辞上留余地。",

This was an **experiment status check worker** that monitored the ongoing progress of 4 depth-contamination experiments running on GPU hardware.

## Key Conclusions from Result JSON

The worker generated a detailed status report (in Chinese) showing:

### GPU Status
- 4 RTX 4090 D GPUs all actively running
- GPU 0-2: 95-96% utilization with 14-16GB VRAM in use
- GPU 3: 58% utilization with 4.5GB VRAM in use
- All 4 tmux sessions alive

### Experiment Progress Summary

| Experiment | GPU | Status | Progress | ETA |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| exp_arXiv_qwen7b | cuda:0 | Generating depth_5 | 68% | ~15 min |
| exp_wiki_mistral7b | cuda:1 | Generating depth_3 | 29% | ~3.3 hours |
| exp_wiki_llama8b | cuda:2 | Generating depth_3 | 36% | ~3 hours |
| exp_arXiv_mistral7b | cuda:3 | Feature extraction (Surprisal) | 85% | ~5-10 min |

### Key Observations
- **exp_arXiv_mistral7b** is leading - all depth generation complete (0-5), now in Surprisal feature extraction stage (94%), expected to finish in minutes
- **exp_arXiv_qwen7b** depth_5 is 78% done, about 60 batches remaining, ~15 minutes to completion
- **Wikipedia experiments** (5000 samples) are slower - each depth takes ~65 minutes vs ~27 for arXiv, still in depth_3 stage with depth_4-5 remaining
- All experiment result directories are currently empty (features.csv and scs_results.json will be generated after all depth chains complete)

The worker successfully completed its status check with no errors.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent! exp_arXiv_mistral7b results:

- **K* = 3** (2v3 AUC = 0.6275 > θ=0.60, 3v4 AUC = 0.5295 < θ=0.60)
- **AUC pattern**: 0v1=0.9848, 1v2=0.8201, 2v3=0.6275, 3v4=0.5295, 4v5=0.5295
- **JSD pattern**: 0v1=0.142, 1v2=0.076, 2v3=0.019, 3v4=0.010, 4v5=0.009


- **5-fold CV**: per-fold variance is stable (1v2 std=0.0147, 2v3 std=0.0076)
- **Feature importance**: Top features are surprisal and frequency-based, consistent with other experiments

The cross-validation results look solid, and this is now the fourth model confirming K*=3 on the arXiv domain—Pythia, LLaMA-8B, and Mistral-7B all converge on the same optimal cluster count.

The JSD profile shows a 1.87x cliff ratio with gradual decay matching the arXiv pattern, and the 2v3 AUC of 0.6275 sits comfortably above the 0.60 threshold with per-fold values ranging from 0.616 to 0.637. Generation quality looks normal too—JSD of 0.142 for 0v1 is nowhere near OLMo's problematic 0.329, so Mistral-7B is generating appropriately domain-specific text. Now I need to verify the GPU allocation—the earlier status showed exp_arXiv_mistral7b on cuda:3, and the project has gpu_ids=[0,1,2,3] available from the loop.

The cuda:3 assignment is valid. This gives us a clean result that really solidifies the arXiv K*=3 finding across models: Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B all converge on K*=3, while OLMo-1B is the only outlier at K*=1 due to capacity constraints. That's four out of five models showing consistent cross-model validation. Let me check what lambda value Mistral-7B ended up with.

Looking at the JSD decay pattern, I'm computing the exponential decay rate λ from the ratios between consecutive JSD values. The calculations show λ ≈ 0.625 from the first transition, then λ ≈ 1.006 from the second transition, which aligns well with the claimed λ≈1.0 from the arXiv paper.

However, when I examine the individual consecutive pairs more carefully, the decay rate isn't actually constant—it varies significantly across transitions, ranging from about 0.1 to 1.4, with the fastest decay occurring between the second and third transitions.
```

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — exp_arXiv_mistral7b\n\n**结论**: PASS\n\n### 核心指标\n- K* = 3（2v3 AUC = 0.6275 > θ=0.60，3v4 = 0.5295 < θ）\n- JSD: [0.142, 0.076, 0.019, 0.010, 0.009]（cliff ratio = 1.87x，符合 arXiv gradual decay 模式）\n- 5-fold CV 稳定：2v3 per-fold = [0.633, 0.630, 0.637, 0.622, 0.616]，全部 > θ=0.60\n- Feature importance top: freq_entropy, surp_std, surp_d1_skew（与其他实验一致）\n- GPU: cuda:3，在白名单 [0,1,2,3] 内 ✓\n\n### 发现\n- [severity: info] 生成质量正常：JSD 0v1=0.142 在正常范围（对比 OLMo arXiv 的 0.329 = capacity failure），Mistral-7B 能生成 domain-appropriate arXiv 文本。\n- [severity: info] arXiv K*=3 跨模型验证现已升至 4 个模型（Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B, LLaMA-8B, Mistral-7B），排除 OLMo 后 100% 一致。这显著加强了论文 \"structured domain K*=3\" 的 claim 强度。\n- [severity: info] 2v3 AUC = 0.6275 margin (+0.0275 over θ) 高于 LLaMA-8B 的 0.6215 (+0.0215)。Mistral-7B（7B scale）在 arXiv 的 K*=3 与 LLaMA-8B（8B scale）一致，进一步排除 scale 是 K*=3 的驱动因素（domain > scale）。\n\n### 对论文的意义\narXiv K*=3 现在有 4 模型支撑（跨 3 个架构家族：Qwen/Pythia/LLaMA/Mistral），是论文最强的跨模型 claim 之一。Discussion 中 \"Domain dependence of K*\" 段落可以更新模型计数。",
